# Conformance report

Date: 2026-11-14. Audited commit: `5e03984ad459c8ed30d11896117b7cd6ebc44752` (all line numbers refer to it). Documents: 117.

Code-only audit (no compile or live testing). Each document was walked section by section (spec-down) and each source file behaviour by behaviour (code-up); rows found by only one sweep were re-audited, then every fragment was verified. Only requirements identified as non-conformant in the audited commit are listed below; later branch status appears in the row notes. Per-section coverage ledgers stay in `target/conformance/`.

Summary counts describe the audited commit and do not include later branch fixes. Row notes track those fixes; `Fixed` means the named branch contains the fix and is not a claim that it has merged. `Documented` is independent of status and means a README, ADR, or compatibility note records the behavior. `Commits` lists the short hashes of the commits on `main` that implement each row (checked against the code; `(partial)` and `(docs only)` mark incomplete or documentation-only resolutions, `—` means no change); `Issue` lists the GitHub issues (#95–#105 conformance packages, #108/#109 proxy source ports) that track or fixed the row, taken from the references in its explanation; `—` means no issue covers it yet.

| §2.2 Common configurations | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/mod.rs:60-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/setup.rs:90-100` | **Partial**: Pharos can retrieve and serve configured secondary zones, but it permits primary zones without a secondary and does not verify that every zone has redundant support. | No | — | — |
| §2.3.4 Size limits | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/key.rs:35,108-156` | **Deviation**: Wire names are limited to 255 octets, but CoreDNS's 255-byte presentation cap is retained for keys; it rejects some RFC-valid escaped names. | §3.1 Name space definitions | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/mod.rs:145-151,173-183` | **Fixed**: Zone input preserves owner and name-bearing RDATA case; comparisons remain ASCII case-insensitive. Fixed on `conf/zone` (#95). | No | #95 | `4d378827` |

RFC 2181 §7.2 only removes the requirement to send SOA records with zero TTL. | Yes | — | — |

| §3.1 Standard policies | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/setup.rs:276-286` | **Partial**: `*` represents all types, but configuration has no concise user-types selector. | No | — | — |
| §4.3 Non-explicit updates to SIGs | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dynupdate/store.rs:66-79`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:32-47` | **Partial**: Dynamic updates commit changed RRsets without signatures. The DNSSEC plugin signs selected responses only after a query with DO; it does not ensure the updated RRset is signed when the update completes. | No | — | — |
| §4.4 Effects on the zone | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/zone/update.rs:184-198`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:116-151` | **Partial**: A changed zone gets a new SOA serial, but the update transaction does not regenerate or sign SOA and denial records. DNSSEC only synthesizes denial NSEC and signs selected records in later DO responses. | No | — | — |

| §8.2 New Temporal Dependency Issues for Zones | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:83-85` | **Fixed**: When Unix time is not newer than the input SOA serial under RFC 1982 arithmetic, the signer advances the existing serial by one (`sign/signer.rs:130-138`). | §3 Minimally Covering NSEC Records | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/denial.rs:159-215`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:117-129` | **Deviation**: Before answering, RFC requires checking the span and using a better epsilon or the closest covered existing name when needed. ## RFC 5625: DNS Proxy Implementation Guidelines

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented | Issue | Commits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| §3 The Transparency Principle | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:182-185`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:407-414` | **Partial**: the proxy normally uses the client's transport, but `prefer_udp` sends TCP-client queries upstream over UDP. The forward README records this CoreDNS-compatible option. | Yes | — | — |
| §4.4.1 TCP Transport | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:407-414` | **Deviation**: With `prefer_udp`, the proxy sends even a TCP-received Query over UDP first, then retries over TCP only if the UDP response has TC. The `forward` README documents this CoreDNS-compatible transport preference. | Yes | — | — |
| §4.4.2 Extension Mechanisms for DNS (EDNS0) | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:57-64`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/edns.rs:26-33` | **Partial**: Unsupported EDNS-version proxying remains deferred because admission is in unowned `pharos-server` paths (conf/server #101). | No | #101 | `7bdc9e60` (partial) |
| §4.5 Secret Key Transaction Authentication for DNS (TSIG) | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/mod.rs:166-178`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/mod.rs:253-265`, `crates/pharos-server/src/outcome.rs:108-147,327-329` | **Fixed**: AD is cleared only after verified client TSIG is stripped and Pharos re-signs without upstream transaction security. Fixed on `conf/forward` (#104). | No | #104 | `19e5cfb9`, `d9cdaa8b` |
| §6.1 Forgery Resilience | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:476-487` | **Deviation**: Upstream queries get unpredictable random IDs (RFC 5452 §9.2), but TSIG-signed Queries with unshared keys keep the client's ID, because RFC 8945 §5.5 requires forwarding them unchanged. The `forward` README documents that TSIG Messages retain their original ID. | Yes | — | — |
| §6.2 Interface Binding | `crates/pharos-corefile/src/setup.rs:61-65`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/lib.rs:399-402`, `crates/pharos-server/src/lib.rs:877-899`, `crates/pharos/src/app.rs:41-43` | **Deviation**: The default listener binds an unspecified dual-stack address and can expose a `forward` proxy on every interface; configure `bind` to restrict it. Recorded in `docs/compatibility.md` (RFC 5625 §6.2; conf/infra #105). | Yes | #105 | `91fcddab` (docs only) |
| §6.3 Packet Filtering | `crates/pharos-server/src/admission.rs:24-47` | **Deviation**: Datagrams or TCP messages shorter than the 12-octet header are dropped (no Message ID to echo; a reply would amplify; CoreDNS/miekg parity). RFC 5625 §6.3 allows dropping (MAY) but prefers a synthesized error (SHOULD). Malformed messages with a complete header receive FORMERR. Changed on `rem/server` (not merged); recorded in `compatibility.md`. | Yes | #101 | `7bdc9e60` (partial); rem/server #101: `4ad3ad53`, `90489e10` |

## RFC 6781: DNSSEC Operational Practices, Version 2

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented | Issue | Commits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| §3.1 Operational Motivation for Zone Signing Keys and Key Signing Keys | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359` | **Fixed**: In split-key mode the signer filters keys so the KSK signs only the apex DNSKEY RRset and the ZSK signs non-apex DNSKEY RRsets (`signing.rs`, RFC 6781 §3.1 comment). The code predates `rem/dnssec`, which added a regression test and recorded the CoreDNS difference in the dnssec README and `compatibility.md`. | Yes | #100 | rem/dnssec #100: `461baadd` (test only), `4eb8996c` (docs only) |
| §3.4.1 Signature Algorithm | `crates/pharos-dnssec/src/keys.rs:67-75` | **Deviation**: RFC 6781 recommends RSA/SHA-1 as an alternative, but Pharos rejects algorithms 5 and 7. The DNSSEC plugin README documents the rejection because RFC 9905 §2 prohibits these algorithms for signing. | Yes | — | — |
| §4.1 Key Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:52-57,87-118,198-224` | **Partial**: The signer replaces DNSKEY/RRSIG input and regenerates records only from the configured keys. It has no rollover stages or cache-window tracking, so changing keys can remove material needed to validate cached data. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.1 Zone Signing Key Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-359` | **Partial**: The online signer separates KSK and ZSK signatures, but does not stage ZSK rollover or retain old keys for the cache interval; configured keys determine the current DNSKEY and signatures. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.1.1 Pre-Publish Zone Signing Key Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/setup.rs:43-70`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:113-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-365` | **Partial**: Every configured key has a private signer, appears in the DNSKEY answer, and signs eligible RRsets immediately. The implementation cannot publish a future ZSK without using it to sign data. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.2 Key Signing Key Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:99-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-365` | **Partial**: Multiple KSKs can be published and can sign the DNSKEY RRset together. The signer does not coordinate parent DS changes or preserve the old KSK through the TTL_DNSKEY and TTL_DS waits required by Verified erratum 4844. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.2.1 Special Considerations for RFC 5011 KSK Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: A revoked KSK remains eligible to sign the DNSKEY RRset, but the implementation does not enforce the additional Maximum Zone TTL publication period before removal. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.3 Single-Type Signing Scheme Key Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: A single configured key can sign both DNSKEY and other RRsets, and multiple keys can overlap. The implementation does not stage the DS change or wait for old DNSKEY/DS data to expire before removing a key. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.4 Algorithm Rollovers | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:113-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-409` | **Partial**: Configuring a new algorithm publishes its DNSKEY and signatures together. The signer cannot first publish new-algorithm signatures, wait for old signed data to expire, then publish the DNSKEY and coordinate the parent DS transition. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.4.1 Single-Type Signing Scheme Algorithm Rollover | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/mod.rs:113-129`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:350-409` | **Partial**: The signer can use multiple algorithms, but publishes each configured DNSKEY and its signatures together. It cannot perform the required signatures-first cache transition for a single-type key. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.4.2 Algorithm Rollover, RFC 5011 Style | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: A revoked key can sign the DNSKEY RRset, but no rollover state ensures the new trust anchor is established before revocation or retains the revoked key for the RFC 5011 period. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.1.4.3 Single Signing Type Algorithm Rollover, RFC 5011 Style | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/dnssec/signing.rs:337-365` | **Partial**: Configured ZSKs can sign zone RRsets while KSKs sign DNSKEY, allowing a shadow-key arrangement. Skipped on `rem/dnssec`: rollover state machines are out of scope. | No | — | — |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Maximum Zone TTL | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:172-183` | **Partial**: The signer sets a 32-day validity period plus up to five days of jitter and preserves source TTLs. | §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Minimum Zone TTL | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:73-85`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:149-155`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/signer.rs:179-183` | **Partial**: The signer accepts zone TTLs and uses them for signed RRsets without checking that the Minimum Zone TTL supports fetching and validating the complete trust chain. | No | — | — |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — SOA expiration timing | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:244-265` | **Partial**: SOA refresh, retry, and expire values are used as supplied. The implementation does not relate SOA expiration to the refresh period or signature validity period. | No | — | — |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Secondary refresh before signature expiration | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:244-265`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:316-357`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/mod.rs:198-204` | **Partial**: The secondary refreshes on the SOA timer and stops serving only after SOA expiration following failed refreshes. It does not compare refresh or expiry timing with RRSIG expiration, so it can serve expired signatures while the SOA expiry timer remains active. | No | — | — |
| §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Signature publication period | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/output.rs:13-46`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/sign/mod.rs:91-100` | **Partial**: The signer checks every five hours and re-signs when a signature has less than seven days remaining. | §4.4.1 Time Considerations — Upcoming signature expiration monitoring | `—` | **Missing**: The secondary has no monitor or alert for upcoming RRSIG expiration; its refresh loop checks SOA serials only. | No | — | — |

With no later traffic, cached TCP sessions remain open indefinitely; the README documents this behavior. | Yes | — | — |
| §6.2.3 Idle Timeouts | `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:243-272, 572-585`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/forward/README.md:10` | **Deviation**: The client keeps idle TCP sessions without an idle-timeout signaling mechanism, and the configured expiry is not enforced without another exchange; the README documents this behavior. | Yes | — | — |
| §6.2.4 Teardown | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:186-189, 280-286, 310` | **Deviation**: A peer FIN is treated as a TCP half-close (RFC 9293 §3.6.1): the server answers Queries it already read, bounded by the TCP idle timeout, although RFC 7766 §6.2.4 says pending Responses MUST NOT be sent once the client has closed. RST or a failed write aborts in-flight Query tasks and Transfers, and FIN cancels running AXFR/IXFR streams (RFC 5936 §4.1.2). Recorded in `crates/pharos/compatibility.md`; changed on `rem/server` (#101). | Yes | #101 | rem/server #101: `e3c4c981`, `fae425d1`, `e1571c13`, `4ef1e977` (docs only) |
| §8 TCP Message Length Field | `crates/pharos-server/src/transfer.rs:165-173`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/secondary/update.rs:181-199` | **Fixed**: The SOA-check, AXFR, and NOTIFY-over-TCP clients write the length prefix and Message together through the shared `pharos_server::write_tcp_frame`, which also rejects a Message above 65,535 octets (RFC 1035 §4.2.2) with `InvalidInput` instead of truncating the length. Fixed on `rem/secondary` (#98). | No | #98 | rem/secondary #98: `7aafda2b`, `cabaac5d` |

## RFC 8777: DNS Reverse IP Automatic Multicast Tunneling (AMT) Discovery

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented | Issue | Commits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| §4.2 RData Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:31,963`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/rdata.rs:1260-1295` | **Partial**: Zone text can encode AMTRELAY, but wire parsing rejects it (AMTRELAY is in `unsupported_name_type`, rdata.rs:31, so rdata.rs:963 returns an error) and the `Unknown` writer rejects it too (rdata.rs:1260-1295). Valid records therefore cannot pass through typed parse/rebuild. | No | — | — |
| §4.2 RData Format | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:163-165` | **Violation**: The zone encoder accepts type values 128–255 and ORs them into the D/type octet, although type is only 7 bits. Values with the high bit set can alter D. Existing RFC 8777 §4 citation covers address encoding, not this bound. | No | — | — |
| §4.2.3 RData Format - Type | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:163-176` | **Violation**: With D=0, types 0–3 use their defined relay encodings (type 3 uncompressed). With D=1, the `out[1] < 128` check at advanced.rs:174 drops the relay bytes for every type, so types 1–3 lose the address or name their type defines. No ADR, plugin README, or `docs/compatibility.md` entry justifies this; zone_advanced.rs:257 asserts the truncated `[10, 0x83]` for type 3. | No | — | — |
| §4.2.4 RData Format - Relay | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:167-176` | **Violation**: With D=0, the empty, IPv4, IPv6, and uncompressed-name relay fields are encoded, including the root label that Erratum 6218 requires. With D=1, advanced.rs:174 omits the relay field for types 1–3, though the field is required for each type. No ADR, plugin README, or `docs/compatibility.md` entry justifies this. | No | — | — |
| §4.3.1 Representation of AMTRELAY RRs | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:146-171` | **Violation**: The parser requires the presentation fields and enforces `.` for type 0, but accepts an 8-bit type value above 127. It then treats the high bit as D, silently changing the supplied type; the relay type is only 7 bits. | No | — | — |
| §5 IANA Considerations | `crates/pharos-proto/src/zone/rdata/advanced.rs:163-165` | **Violation**: Verified Erratum 6688 makes Relay Type a 7-bit field, but parsing accepts 128–255 and ORs those bits into D; input 128 is silently encoded as D=1, Relay Type=0. The nearby citation names §4, not §5. | No | — | — |

Skipped on `rem/fwdtsig`: the unsigned-stream API is kept for legacy interoperability. | No | — | — |

Changed on `rem/fwdtsig` (not merged); recorded in the TSIG README and `compatibility.md`. | Yes | — | rem/fwdtsig: `00f038a9`, `88d6f8ee`, `b5ec6220` (docs only) |

Fixed on `conf/forward` (#104). Skipped on `rem/fwdtsig`: per-key algorithm policy not implemented. | No | — | — |

Fixed on `rem/fwdtsig` (not merged). | No | — | rem/fwdtsig: `c3fada09`, `287fe112` (test only) |
| §8 Shared Secrets | `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig_key.rs:33-45`, `crates/pharos-proto/src/tsig.rs:68-79`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/tsig/setup.rs:99-108` | **Deviation**: Any decodable secret length is still accepted for CoreDNS compatibility, but setup now logs INFO when a decoded secret is shorter than 64 octets (the longest HMAC output), addressing the §8 SHOULD by warning. Changed on `rem/fwdtsig` (not merged); recorded in the TSIG README and `compatibility.md`. | Yes | — | rem/fwdtsig: `00f038a9`, `b5ec6220` (docs only) |

## RFC 9293: Transmission Control Protocol (TCP)

| Section | Code location | Explanation | Documented | Issue | Commits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| §3.7.4 Nagle Algorithm | `crates/pharos-server/src/tcp.rs:112`, `crates/pharos-plugins/src/proxy/mod.rs:70` | **Violation**: SHLD-7 says a TCP implementation SHOULD implement Nagle. Both the DNS-over-TCP server and the proxy upstream always call `set_nodelay(true)` and never enable Nagle. The per-connection disable (MUST-17) is met by the same call. No ADR or plugin README justifies the deviation; the `tcp.rs:111` comment gives a rationale but is not a recorded decision. Skipped on `rem/http`: Nagle behavior is out of scope. | Yes | — | — |
